Protocol-Based Dispatch (Python's duck typing)\n**Python:** `isinstance()` checks with `Tokenizable` protocol\n**Scala:**\n- Use sealed trait hierarchies with pattern matching\n- Leverage implicit type classes for stateless tokenizers\n- Use method overloading for simple cases\n\n### 6. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01CT542PsZimZahRhtcsgTWW","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01VqQCM7YiED2PZTj3f2DT3r","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/Tokenizer.scala","content":"/**\n * Tokenizer module for converting various input types to standardized string tokens.\n *\n * This Scala translation leverages:\n * - Sealed trait hierarchies for type-safe unions and pattern matching\n * - Case classes for immutable data structures with structural equality\n * - Type variance annotations for generic containers\n * - Pattern matching for type-based dispatch\n * - Scala standard library for collections and type handling\n *\n * Scala 2.13 compatible\n */\n\nimport scala.collection.immutable.{Map, Vector, List}\nimport scala.math.Numeric\nimport scala.annotation.implicitNotFound\nimport java.time.{LocalDate, LocalDateTime, ZonedDateTime}\nimport java.time.format.DateTimeFormatter\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets\nimport scala.util.{Try, Success, Failure}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Type Definition & Sealed Hierarchies\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n * Sealed trait representing different token types.\n * Replaces Python's Enum with more Scala-idiomatic sealed trait hierarchy.\n */\nsealed trait TokenType {\n  def value: String\n}\n\ncase object StringToken extends TokenType { val value = \"string\" }\ncase object NumericToken extends TokenType { val value = \"numeric\" }\ncase object TemporalToken extends TokenType { val value = \"temporal\" }\ncase object StructuredToken extends TokenType { val value = \"structured\" }\ncase object BinaryToken extends TokenType { val value = \"binary\" }\ncase object NullToken extends TokenType { val value = \"null\" }\n\n// Alternative enum-like accessor (for compatibility)\nobject TokenType {\n  def STRING: TokenType = StringToken\n  def NUMERIC: TokenType = NumericToken\n  def TEMPORAL: TokenType = TemporalToken\n  def STRUCTURED: TokenType = StructuredToken\n  def BINARY: TokenType = BinaryToken\n  def NULL: TokenType = NullToken\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Core Token & Batch Classes\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n * Immutable token representation.\n * Case class provides structural equality, pattern matching, and copy semantics.\n */\ncase class Token(\n  value: String,\n  tokenType: TokenType,\n  metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) {\n  /**\n   * Return new token with additional metadata, preserving existing entries.\n   */\n  def withMetadata(pairs: (String, Any)*): Token =\n    this.copy(metadata = this.metadata ++ pairs.toMap)\n}\n\n/**\n * Mutable batch of tokens for accumulation scenarios.\n * Contrasts with immutable Token design.\n */\nclass TokenBatch private (\n  private var tokens: Vector[Token] = Vector.empty,\n  private var _processed: Boolean = false\n) {\n  def add(token: Token): Unit = {\n    if (_processed) throw new IllegalStateException(\"Batch already processed\")\n    tokens = tokens :+ token\n  }\n\n  def markProcessed(): Unit = _processed = true\n\n  def isProcessed: Boolean = _processed\n\n  def getTokens: Vector[Token] = tokens.toVector\n\n  def size: Int = tokens.length\n}\n\nobject TokenBatch {\n  def apply(): TokenBatch = new TokenBatch()\n  def apply(initial: Vector[Token]): TokenBatch = new TokenBatch(initial)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Protocol/Structural Typing Traits\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n * Trait for any object that can be converted to a token string.\n * Replaces Python's Protocol class with Scala trait.\n */\ntrait Tokenizable {\n  def toToken: String\n}\n\n/**\n * Trait for objects with length capability.\n */\ntrait HasLength {\n  def length: Int\n}\n\n/**\n * Trait for type-safe token processing with contravariance.\n * The -T indicates contravariance: can process supertypes.\n */\ntrait TokenProcessor[-T] {\n  def process(item: T): Unit\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Generic Container Classes with Variance\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n * Covariant container - can return subtypes.\n * The +T indicates covariance: safe to return subtypes.\n */\nclass TokenContainer[+T](items: Seq[T]) {\n  private val _items: Vector[T] = items.toVector\n\n  def getAll: Vector[T] = _items\n\n  def mapTokens[S](func: T => S): Vector[S] =\n    _items.map(func)\n\n  def size: Int = _items.length\n\n  def isEmpty: Boolean = _items.isEmpty\n\n  def iterator: Iterator[T] = _items.iterator\n}\n\nobject TokenContainer {\n  def apply[T](items: Seq[T]): TokenContainer[T] = new TokenContainer(items)\n  def empty[T]: TokenContainer[T] = new TokenContainer[T](Vector.empty)\n}\n\n/**\n * Contravariant sink - can accept supertypes.\n * The -T indicates contravariance: can accept supertypes.\n */\nclass TokenSink[-T] {\n  private var _received: Vector[T] = Vector.empty\n\n  def receive(item: T): Unit = {\n    _received = _received :+ item\n  }\n\n  def drain(): Vector[T] = {\n    val result = _received\n    _received = Vector.empty\n    result\n  }\n\n  def size: Int = _received.length\n}\n\nobject TokenSink {\n  def apply[T](): TokenSink[T] = new TokenSink[T]\n}\n\n/**\n * Invariant handler - exact type matching required.\n * No variance annotation: can both read and write, so must be invariant.\n */\nclass BivariantHandler[T](initial: T) {\n  private var _value: T = initial\n\n  def get: T = _value\n\n  def set(value: T): Unit = {\n    _value = value\n  }\n\n  def transform(func: T => T): T = {\n    _value = func(_value)\n    _value\n  }\n}\n\nobject BivariantHandler {\n  def apply[T](initial: T): BivariantHandler[T] = new BivariantHandler(initial)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Base Tokenizer Trait & Implementations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n * Abstract base trait for tokenizers with generic input type.\n */\nsealed trait Tokenizer[T] {\n  /**\n   * Convert value to token.\n   */\n  def tokenize(value: T): Token\n\n  /**\n   * Lazy tokenization of multiple values using Iterator.\n   */\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[T]): Iterator[Token] =\n    values.iterator.map(tokenize)\n}\n\n/**\n * Tokenizer for string and bytes types.\n */\nclass StringTokenizer(\n  encoding: String = \"utf-8\",\n  normalizer: String => String = identity\n) extends Tokenizer[String] {\n\n  def tokenize(value: String): Token = {\n    val normalized = normalizer(value)\n    Token(normalized, StringToken)\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Specialized overload for bytes for Double tokenization.\n   */\n  def tokenizeDouble(value: Double): Token =\n    tokenize(Right(Left(value)))\n\n  /**\n   * Convenience method for BigDecimal tokenization.\n   */\n  def tokenizeDecimal(value: java.math.BigDecimal): Token =\n    tokenize(Right(Right(value)))\n}\n\nobject NumericTokenizer {\n  def apply(precision: Int = 6, formatOptions: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty): NumericTokenizer =\n    new NumericTokenizer(precision, formatOptions)\n}\n\n/**\n * Tokenizer for date/time types.\n */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatStr: Option[String] = None) extends Tokenizer[Either[LocalDateTime, LocalDate]] {\n\n  private val isoFormatter = DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern(\"yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss\")\n  private val dateFormatter = DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern(\"yyyy-MM-dd\")\n  private val customFormatter = formatStr.map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern(_))\n\n  def tokenize(value: Either[LocalDateTime, LocalDate]): Token = {\n    val format = customFormatter.getOrElse {\n      value match {\n        case Left(_) => isoFormatter\n        case Right(_) => dateFormatter\n      }\n    }\n\n    val strValue = value match {\n      case Left(dt) => dt.format(format)\n      case Right(d) => d.format(format)\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TemporalToken)\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Convenience method for LocalDateTime tokenization.\n   */\n  def tokenizeDateTime(value: LocalDateTime): Token =\n    tokenize(Left(value))\n\n  /**\n   * Convenience method for LocalDate tokenization.\n   */\n  def tokenizeDate(value: LocalDate): Token =\n    tokenize(Right(value))\n}\n\nobject TemporalTokenizer {\n  def apply(formatStr: Option[String] = None): TemporalTokenizer =\n    new TemporalTokenizer(formatStr)\n\n  def apply(formatStr: String): TemporalTokenizer =\n    new TemporalTokenizer(Some(formatStr))\n}\n\n/**\n * Whitespace tokenizer - basic text tokenization.\n *\n * Fundamental tokenization operation used in NLP and text processing.\n */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase: Boolean = false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n   * Process a single word into a token string.\n   */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var processed = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n      processed = processed.dropWhile(punctuation.contains)\n                           .reverse\n                           .dropWhile(punctuation.contains)\n                           .reverse\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      processed = processed.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (processed.length < minLength) {\n      return None\n    }\n\n    if (maxLength.exists(processed.length > _)) {\n      processed = processed.take(maxLength.get)\n    }\n\n    if (processed.nonEmpty) Some(processed) else None\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Split text by whitespace and return list of tokens.\n   */\n  def tokenize(text: String): Vector[Token] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n    words.zipWithIndex\n      .flatMap { case (word, idx) =>\n      .toVector\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Split text by whitespace and return list of token strings.\n   */\n  def tokenizeToStrings(text: String): Vector[String] =\n    tokenize(text).map(_.value)\n\n  /**\n   * Tokenize text and return tokens with character positions.\n   */\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): Vector[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    val result = word.length\n\n      processToken(word).foreach { processed =>\n        result += ((processed, start, end))\n      }\n\n      currentPos = end\n    }\n\n    result.toVector\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Return stripPunctuation)\n}\n\n/**\n * Universal tokenizer that handles multiple types via pattern matching.\n *\n * Replaces Python's duck-typing dispatch with Scala's pattern matching\n * and sealed trait temporalTokenizer = TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n   * Dispatch to appropriate tokenizer based on runtime type.\n   * Pattern matching provides exhaustive checking at compile time.\n   */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case null =>\n      Token(\"NULL\", NullToken)\n\n    case t: Tokenizable =>\n      Token(t.toToken, StructuredToken)\n\n    case s: String =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(s)\n\n    case b: Array[Byte] =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenizeBytes(b)\n\n    case i: Int =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenizeInt(i)\n\n    case d: Double =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenizeDouble(d)\n\n    case bd: java.math.BigDecimal =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenizeDecimal(bd)\n\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenizeDateTime(dt)\n\n    case d: LocalDate =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenizeDate(d)\n\n    case m: Map[_, _] =>\n      // JSON-like map handling\n      val jsonStr = m.toString\n      Token(jsonStr, StructuredToken, Map(\"type\" -> \"map\"))\n\n    case s: Seq[_] =>\n      // JSON-like sequence handling\n      val jsonStr = s.toString\n      Token(jsonStr, StructuredToken, Map(\"type\" -> \"seq\"))\n\n    case other =>\n      // Fallback: try string conversion\n      Token(other.toString, StringToken, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n  }\n}\n\nobject UniversalTokenizer {\n  def apply(): UniversalTokenizer = new UniversalTokenizer()\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Advanced Patterns: Registry with Generic Types\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n * Registry with complex nested generic types.\n *\n * Demonstrates pattern for managing .find(_.isDefined)\n            .flatten\n        }\n    }\n  }\n\n  def getRegistry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = registry\n\n  def getHandlers: Vector[T => Option[Token]] = handlers\n}\n\nobject processing.\n */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase: Boolean = false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n   * Process a single word into a token string.\n   */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var processed = word\n\n           .reverse\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      processed = processed.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (processed.length < minLength) {\n      return None\n    }\n\n    if and return list of tokens.\n   */\n  def tokenize(text: String): Vector[Token] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n    words.zipWithIndex\n      .flatMap { case (word, idx) =>\n /**\n   * Tokenize text and return tokens with character positions.\n   */\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): Vector[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    val result = scala.collection.mutable.Vector[(String, Int, Int)]()\n    var currentPos = 0\n\n    for (word <- text.split(\"\\\\s+\")) {\n      val start = text.indexOf(word, currentPos)\n      val end = start + word.length\n\n      processToken(word).foreach { processed =>\n        result += ((processed, start, end))\n      }\n\n      currentPos = end\n    }\n\n    result.toVector\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Return temporalTokenizer = TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n   * Dispatch to appropriate tokenizer based on runtime type.\n   * Pattern matching provides exhaustive checking at compile time.\n   */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case null =>\n      Token(\"NULL\", NullToken)\n\n    case t: Tokenizable =>\n      Token(t.toToken, StructuredToken)\n\n    case s: String =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(s)\n\n    case b: Array[Byte] =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenizeBytes(b)\n\n    case i: Int =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenizeInt(i)\n\n    case d: Double =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenizeDouble(d)\n\n    case bd: java.math.BigDecimal =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenizeDecimal(bd)\n\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenizeDateTime(dt)\n\n    case d: LocalDate =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenizeDate(d)\n\n    case m: Map[_, _] =>\n      // JSON-like map handling\n      val jsonStr = with Generic Types\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n * Registry with complex nested generic types.\n *\n * Demonstrates pattern for managing registries of typed containers\n * in Scala's type system.\n */\nclass TokenRegistry[T](\n  initialRegistry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = Map.empty,\n  initialHandlers: Vector[T => Option[Token]] = Vector.empty\n) {\n\n  private var registry = initialRegistry\n  private var handlers = initialHandlers\n\n  /**\n   * Register a container under a key.\n   */\n  def register(key: String, container: TokenContainer[T]): Unit = {\n    registry = registry + (key -> container)\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Add a handler function that processes items.\n   */\n  def addHandler(handler: T => .find(_.isDefined)\n            .flatten\n        }\n    }\n  }\n\n  def getRegistry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = registry\n\n  def getHandlers: Vector[T => Option[Token]] = handlers\n}\n\nobject Monad\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n * Functor trait for tokens - provides map operation.\n */\nsealed trait TokenFunctor[T] {\n  def NumericTokenizer(\n  precision: Int = 6,\n  formatOptions: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) extends Tokenizer[Either[Int, Either[Double, java.math.BigDecimal]]] {\n\n  def tokenize(value: Either[Int, punctuation = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n   * Process a single word into a token string.\n   */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var processed = word\n\n Mutable vs Immutable Collections\n**Python:** Mixes mutable and immutable collections\n**Scala:**\n- Prefer immutable by default (Vector, List, Map)\n- Use mutable.Buffer for batch collections when needed\n- Use sealed trait hierarchies for algebraic data types\n\n### 5. Protocol-Based Dispatch (Python's duck typing)\n**Python:** `isinstance()` checks with `Tokenizable` protocol\n**Scala:**\n- Use sealed trait hierarchies with pattern matching\n- Leverage implicit type classes for stateless tokenizers\n- Use method overloading for simple cases\n\n### 6.
